# Errata and gaps found in the paper

Problems found in A. M. Masullo, *The Music of the Primes*, while turning it into Lean. They
refer to the draft of 20 November 2026 (sha256 in [`paper/README.md`](paper/README.md)).

Most entries were first reported by an AI agent. The **Checked** column says whether someone has
since read the passage and agrees: *integrator* means the integrating agent compared the report
with the page; *reviewed* means that two further agents, one after the other, recomputed it
against the page image, the second with the task of refuting the first; *Lean* means that the
refutation is a proof checked by Lean that rests on no statement still unproved (see the end of
this file); *reported* means nobody has yet. An
entry marked *withdrawn* or *moved* did not survive the second check as it stood; it keeps its
number and says what became of it. No entry has been reviewed by a specialist. The
dispute over the main theorem is described in the [README](README.md#concerns-about-the-main-theorem), not here.

The author's preface says that he and Brian Conrad know of "several glitches here and there"
still being fixed, and that the draft is unfinished. Most entries are in the appendices
and early chapters that recall standard material. Since the second wave (Appendix B.4–B.6 and
§5.2, entries F19–F44, I100–I135 and M8–M16; then §5.2.2 to §7.1.4, entries F45–F104, I136–I218
and M17–M20) many concern the paper's own constructions: the proof of the structure theorem for
the spaces of adelic θ-functions (Theorem 5.2.1.1) does not work as printed, the explicit
formulas of §B.4, which Chapter 12 quotes, need their local terms and normalizations recomputed,
and several steps of Chapters 5–7 have no known repair (listed next).

The third batch (the rest of Chapter 7 and the first sections of Chapters 10–12: entries
F105–F173, I219–I310 and M21–M25; then §10.3–10.4 and §11.1.2: F177–F206, I314–I351 and
M26–M28) reaches the trace formula. The bases and nuclear presentations
that the spectral and orbital traces are computed from (§7.3–7.4, §10.2, §11.2) are not
established by the printed arguments, and the comparison of the global with the S-local trace
(§12.3) is a heading with a note to include the section.

On 22 November every row then marked *reviewed*, 389 in all, was given to a second agent whose
task was to refute it against the page image. It rejected six (three before they were
published, and F66, F79 and I184, which are withdrawn below), moved ten from one table to the
other, and corrected the quotation, the counterexample or the Lean reference of sixty-seven
more. One of the rejected rows had misread a pair of parentheses on the page. Batches added
since then go through the same second check before they are published.

The 118 older rows that only their finder, or one further agent, had looked at were then
checked twice in the same way. Four were withdrawn (I14, I19, I30, I53). Twenty-six turned out
to be false statements and not gaps in an argument, and moved to the first table as F207–F232;
four of these had been readers' reports about parts that are now stated in Lean. Fifty-five
others were corrected.

Three outside audits were then compared with this list report by report: Dev Goyal's audit,
Eric Naslund's review, and the audit of Chapters 11–12 filed as issue #2469. Where an entry
here reports the same thing it now names them (93 entries). What they report that was not
here was tested against the page images by two agents in turn; what held is entries
F233–F248, I352–I373, M29–M49 and, for parts of the paper that are not stated in Lean,
R10–R30. Forty wrong cross-references are listed separately near the end.

The sections that were stated last (§1.4, §11.2.1–11.3, the global kernels of §12.1.1, §12.3–12.5
and Chapter 13) went through the same two checks on 22 November: entries F249–F267, I374–I403
and M50. Five of the steps they concern have no known repair and are in the table below.

What each outside reader reported, page by page, and where it stands here, is indexed in
[`OUTSIDE_REPORTS.md`](OUTSIDE_REPORTS.md).

The statements of the first wave (Appendices A, C and D and Chapters 2–4 and 9) were written
before the project reviewed statements independently. On 23 November each of them was compared
with the page images. Where the Lean was found to state something other than the print, a
second agent tried to refute the finding, and a third tried to refute each resulting row. In
29 places the Lean had corrected the paper without saying so: these are entries F268–F277 and
I404–I431, and the declarations concerned now say so in their docstrings. In one place the
Lean was wrong and the paper right; it is in the last table of this file.

## Where no repair is known yet

Most entries below come with a repair, and the Lean states the repaired form. For these the
agents that checked the entries found no repair, and later results use them. That is their
assessment, not a specialist's; each is a candidate for a recorded gap (`AGENTS.md`, "False
statements and gaps") unless someone proves it, repairs it or refutes it.

Sixteen of the twenty-eight rows have since been settled in Lean, and each of those says so in
bold and points to the next section. The twelve that are still open are the project's open
problems: each is an issue labelled `open-problem`, which nobody holds and anyone may work on,
and [#7349](https://github.com/mccorvie/riemann-masullo/issues/7349) lists them. For four of the
twelve the missing step is named in Lean: the cancellation in Theorem 12.5.1.1, on which the
target theorems rest in Lean, and, for Theorem 7.3.3.4, Proposition 10.2.2.2 and Theorem
10.3.2.1, statements that the targets do not use (one, three and one). For two more (§10.4 and
(12.5.1.4)) the part that the manuscript states precisely is in Lean without proof, in files
that nothing imports: `Isolated.spectralStripConvolution_hilbertSchmidt_of_le_one` and
`Isolated.spectralStripGeometricKernel_blocks_bddAbove` for §10.4, and
`Isolated.exists_projectiveKernel_stripTestAction_spectralProjection` for (12.5.1.4). On
24 November contributors narrowed these statements further (#7413, #7425, #7427, #7435, #7444,
#7468, #7470); the rows say how. The
remaining six rows are proof steps that the manuscript does not state precisely enough to
formalize; for Theorem 7.1.2.3 the attempt to state it (#7383) shows why.

| Item | Entries | What is missing | Used later on pp. |
|---|---|---|---|
| Theorem 5.2.1.1(1), p. 230: the S-local θ-quotients are strict LF-spaces | I123 | The printed proof fails. Its companion claim (3), that each finite stage embeds in the global quotient, has since been proved in Lean by a different route (#4511: a continuous left inverse on the one-place quotient, built by averaging over the new local units; see “Steps since proved” below). Strictness has now been proved as well (#7176). The rest of §5.2 (completeness, the strict presentation, the Montel properties) is proved from it. **Since proved in Lean by another route (#4511, #7176): see “Steps since proved” below.** | the rest of §5.2, and what later chapters take from it |
| Lemma 5.3.1.5, p. 242: closed embeddings on the θ-quotients | I138, I139 | Closedness of ran P + ker Φ and continuity of the inverse on the image, for both quotients. Theorem 6.3.2.2 cites the lemma, so its description of the range cannot justify the lemma. In Lean the lemma is reduced (#7213, #7258, #7284) to two statements on the stage with one finite place removed. The first, an identity for the valuation shells of a θ-null test, is proved (#7307). The second, that the difference of the identity and a norm-changing translation is open onto its image on θ-classes, is reduced (#7311, #7329, #7331) to a boundedness comparison for zeta integrals, with the decomposition of the θ-quotient by finite conductor proved on the way (#7331). The comparison is proved (#7346) from a theorem of R. Meyer on the coinvariants of the S-units, which the Lean states as a classical input and does not prove. **Since proved in Lean from a published theorem that the Lean takes as known (#7346): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 309–310, 342, 371, 484–485 |
| Theorem 5.4.1.1, (5.4.1.19)–(5.4.1.20), pp. 272–273: from finite principal parts to the full θ-quotient | I168 | A lift of compatible principal parts in the original topology, or an independent proof of surjectivity. **Since proved in Lean by another route (#7212, #7190): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 273–274, 276–277, 280–282, 296, 299, 302, 305, 309, 348–350, 362, 364–366, 382, 390, 420 |
| Theorem 6.3.2.1(1), (6.3.2.11)–(6.3.2.13), pp. 305–306: gluing the two half-plane pieces, and the closed global Mellin image | I198 | Agreement after weighting repairs the overlap identity. The common transform with the claimed Schwartz bounds, the topological embedding and the equality of images remain to be proved. **Since proved in Lean by another route (#7218, #7263): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 307–310, 326–329, 331–332, 339–340, 379, 441–442, 465, 481, 484, 486 |
| Theorem 6.3.2.1(1), p. 308: division by the outside Euler product | I200 | Holomorphy and rapid decay of both reconstructed half-plane functions on the required strips. The reciprocal has poles outside the critical strip. **Since proved in Lean by another route (#6673, #7196, #7270): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 309–310, 326–329, 331–332, 339, 441–442, 465, 486 |
| Theorem 7.1.2.3, pp. 318, 322–323: convergence of the separately indexed series | I210 | Bounds for the separate sums, uniform on the claimed sets of test functions. The contour argument controls only a common cutoff. A search for the weakest form the later pages need met an arithmetic condition (report on #7279). For $F=\mathbf Q(i)$, the places over 3 and 5 and a character with angular component $(z/\lvert z\rvert)^4$ at infinity, the series at the place over 3 converges absolutely for every test of an explicit family if and only if $\lvert 1-\xi\,e^{-2\pi i m\log 5/\log 9}\rvert\ge c(1+\lvert m\rvert)^{-d}$ for all $m$ at which the left side is not zero, with $\xi=((2+i)/\sqrt5)^{-4}$. For characters of finite order the corresponding bound follows from Baker's theorem; for this one no proof is known to us. Page 389 states the absolute convergence of the sums for each prime as a consequence of Theorem 7.3.1.6. An attempt to state the separately summed expansion in Lean (#7383, not merged) shows that the manuscript does not determine it: the printed expansion counts a pole shared by two places under each of them (F88), and gives no rule for assigning such a pole to one place, on which the convergence of the separate sums can depend. The expansion is now written in Lean as a proposition that takes the assignment of shared poles as a parameter and asserts nothing (`Isolated.sLocalHomogeneousAdelicTheta_separatePlaceExpansionFor`, #7458, in a file that nothing imports). Proved there: every pole can be assigned to one of its places; whenever the separate sums converge for an assignment, their total is the joint limit of the contour argument, so any two convergent assignments give the same total; and changing the assignment at finitely many poles changes nothing. Which assignment the manuscript intends, and whether the sums converge for it, remain open. | 325–326, 333, 349–350, 381, 389–390 |
| Theorem 7.3.1.2(1), p. 341: the range of the S-local principal value equals the pole bound | I226 | The reverse inclusion on every component: a test in the isotype that realizes the divided transform. Vanishing of the high Laurent coefficients only permits division for meromorphic germs. **Since proved in Lean by another route (with Theorem 6.3.2.1(1), itself proved since): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 341–342, 346–349, 360–364; the Fourier basis on 374, 376–378, 381–384 |
| Theorem 7.3.1.6(2)–(3), pp. 345, 349–350: arbitrary enumerations of the polar system are Schauder bases | I230 | Control of the individual coordinate projections that is uniform across the coordinates at a repeated pole, or an arithmetic counterexample. **Since proved in Lean from another step of this list (Lemma 5.3.1.5): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 350, 360–364, 381–382, 389, 393–394, 496, 776 |
| Theorem 7.3.3.4, pp. 360–363: the Paley–Wiener orbital bases and their lifts | F127, I242 | At the trivial character the missing Fourier coordinate cannot pair to one, so a basis of the surviving jets, with biorthogonal vectors and estimates, is needed. On every component, a lift of the compact-window quotient with one Fourier bound common to all tests. In Lean the single-place lift is proved (#7198, #7260), and so is Fourier synthesis on the primary fibre (#7310). Bounded interpolation is reduced (#7197, #7269, #7312, #7330) to an estimate that separates the pole lattices of two different rational primes: at the repeated poles of the Euler factors above one prime, the Euler factor of a place above the other is bounded by a polynomial in the height of the pole. The estimate is proved in the elementary cases, and for characters of finite order from the theorem of Baker and Wüstholz on linear forms in two logarithms, of which the Lean states the case of multiplicatively independent integers as a classical input and does not prove it (`baker_two_logarithms_not_liouville_of_independent`). For a character of infinite order it needs a polynomial lower bound for a linear form in two logarithms shifted by the phases of the character; Baker's theorem covers the unshifted form only, and page 349 supplies no bound. This has been narrowed (#7413). Over a number field of degree at most two no repeated pole of this kind exists, and nothing is open there. In degree three and above, comparing two poles cancels the shifts, and the classical input bounds every pole that has a competitor at least as close. What is left is the recorded gap `sLocalPWOrbital_bases_bounded_interpolation_laurent_other_prime_shift_minima` (#7336): the same bound along an infinite sequence of record closest approaches whose heights more than square at each step. No proof is known to us. An example is worked out in Lean over ℚ(ζ₈), with two places above 17 (#7451, in a file that nothing imports): one of them has infinitely many eligible repeated-pole centers with unbounded Euler values and no clearing shift, and a mixed inequality for the two places together is proved from the Baker–Wüstholz theorem for three fixed logarithms, stated as a classical input (`bakerWustholz_fixedThreeLogs`, #7487). The estimate for a single place stays open. The theorem also rests on Lemma 5.3.1.5. | 361–364, 366–367, 382, 393–394, 489–491, 495–496 |
| Theorem 7.4.1.1(2), (7.4.1.2), pp. 366–367: strong nuclearity on the spectral quotient | I246 | An equicontinuous family of functionals and a bounded family of vectors in the actual quotient, to go with the bounds on the zero counts and coefficients, or another argument for order zero. **Since proved in Lean by another route (#7065): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 367, 394, 452 |
| Theorem 7.4.2.1(2), p. 367: nuclearity on the prime-orbital quotient | I247 | A nuclear presentation, or a controlled periodic model, of the polar quotient. The cited result gives comparison jets without the needed bounds. **Since proved in Lean from another step of this list (Lemma 5.3.1.5): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 392–393, 490, 494–496, 514 |
| Theorem 7.4.3.1(2)–(5), (7.4.3.11), pp. 371–372: the archimedean jets as a basis | I252 | The relative ideal comparison on the θ-numerator, equality of the flat Mellin image with the pulled-back finite ideal, and a realization of the adjusted jet families with the quotient topology. **Since proved in Lean by another route (with Theorem 6.3.2.1(1), itself proved since): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 372–373, 390–393, 514 |
| Theorem 7.5.0.1, pp. 376–378: transfer of the basis, and surjectivity | I258 | A polar basis with continuous synthesis, or an independent proof of surjectivity. An inverse on the regular blocks does not detect the exceptional polar class. (The paper sets §7.5 outside the prerequisites of the main trace calculation, p. 374.) **Since proved in Lean by another route (with Theorem 6.3.2.1(1), itself proved since): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 378–379, 381–384, 387–391 |
| Theorems 10.2.1.1 and 10.2.2.1, pp. 441–443, 446: the spectral Markushevich bases | F155, I274, I275 | Equality of the closed θ-image with the common kernel of the jets, and a biorthogonal vector for each jet, with dense span. The printed proof of both density claims uses the adjoint range of the finite-band projector, in which the in-band evaluations need not lie. **Since proved in Lean by another route (#6465): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 444, 446–455, 458–473, 484–487, 519–520, 522 |
| Remark 10.2.1.5, p. 444, and Proposition 10.2.2.2, pp. 446–447: normalized bases and the uniform bound on strips | F158, I277 | Closely spaced zeros on one isotype rule out uniformly bounded primal vectors, and the strip bound needs a bound on the orders of the jets and a margin from the boundary. A trace estimate that handles clusters of zeros, high orders and the edges of the strip would replace both. In Lean the estimate is reduced (#7191) to one statement, `spectralStripJet_order_and_margin_locally_finite_multiple_zeros`: near any real number only finitely many pairs of real part and multiplicity occur among the multiple zeros. That statement is proved (#7425) from two recorded gaps, its two halves in a global form: `spectralStripJet_order_and_margin_multiplicity_bounded` (#7220), one integer bounds the multiplicity of every zero of every chosen L-function, and `spectralStripJet_order_and_margin_multiple_zero_lines_finite`, the multiple zeros lie on finitely many vertical lines. The second follows from the paper's main claim, which the proposition stands on the way to. It is in turn proved equivalent (#7470) to two recorded gaps together: `spectralStripJet_multiple_zero_bounded_height_representatives` (#7453), for each character every line of multiple zeros has a zero at bounded height, and `spectralStripJet_multiple_real_zeros_finite` (#7485), for each choice of basepoints only finitely many multiple zeros of the family lie on the real segment (1/2, 1). The first does not: over ℚ it contains a bound, independent of the height, for the multiplicities of the zeros of the Riemann zeta function, which is open. | 447, 450–455, 461–464, 468–469, 472–473, 486–489, 519–520 |
| Theorems 10.3.1.1(1) and 10.3.2.1(1), (10.3.1.23), (10.3.2.1), pp. 448, 452–455: trace-norm convergence of the rectangular partial sums | I314 | A trace-class limit obtained by grouping frequencies does not bound a partial sum that cuts through a cluster of zeros. Estimates in the actual norms are needed for arbitrary rectangles and for strips of positive width. In Lean the line theorem is derived from the strip theorem at width zero (#7272, after #6383), and both rest on one statement, `spectralStripConvolution_traceCauchy_compact_block_series` (#7208): the block traces against any fixed compact operator are summable over the zeros. It is a recorded gap (#7241). A sufficient statement is isolated in a file that nothing imports (#7435), `Isolated.spectralStripConvolution_summable_jetNorms` (#7455): a summable majorant built from the Mellin transform of the test and the norms of the coordinate functionals and of their biorthogonal vectors. The gap is proved from it. It asks for more than the gap, and it is not known to hold. The majorant is proved equivalent (#7468) to `Isolated.spectralStripConvolution_finiteGram_bound` (#7455): one constant bounds the Mellin-weighted sums of the diagonal entries of the inverses of the finite Gram matrices of the weighted jets. What is not known is how those entries grow when zeros lie close together. | 452–455, 458–464, 466–473, 489–491, 496, 503, 519–520, 522 |
| Theorems 10.4.2.1, 10.4.2.2 and 10.4.2.8, pp. 459–464, and Theorem 10.4.3.5, pp. 471–473: nuclearity on the quotient, and the trace of convolution after projection | I328, F191, I332, I334, M26 | Nuclearity on the quotient (the line and strip inputs require s > 1); an estimate that allows clusters of zeros in place of the uniform collar bound (10.4.2.24); a trace-norm Cauchy estimate for the finite sums; and control of the geometric output of convolution after projection, or a proof that $\mathrm{Tr}(M_\rho GAP)\to0$. The author's footnote 8 marks this proof as unfinished. In Lean, for s > 1 the results of §10.4.2 follow from Theorem 10.3.2.1 (#7286), including $\mathrm{Tr}(M_\rho GAP)\to0$; the range 0 ≤ s ≤ 1 and the projective Cauchy estimate that pp. 471–473 need are not covered. Those two are stated in Lean without proof, in files that nothing imports (#7388): `Isolated.spectralStripConvolution_nuclear_of_le_one` (nuclearity on the strip quotient for 0 ≤ s ≤ 1; it is proved for width zero, and the conclusions of Theorems 10.4.2.1, 10.4.2.2 and 10.4.2.8 at those orders are proved from it; since #7444 it is itself proved from `Isolated.spectralStripConvolution_hilbertSchmidt_of_le_one` (#7405), that every test acts on the strip quotient by a Hilbert–Schmidt operator at these orders, and the Dixmier–Malliavin factorization of Bruhat tests on the idele classes, stated as a classical input, `Isolated.bruhatCompactSupport_convolution_factorization` (#7486); an argument for the Hilbert–Schmidt statement, for every width and with no hypothesis on the zeros, is posted on #7405 as mathematics and has not been checked in Lean) and, for the Cauchy estimate for the geometric part of the kernel of Theorem 10.4.3.5 (`Isolated.spectralStripGeometricKernel_cauchy`, proved equivalent to the existence of that kernel), `Isolated.spectralStripGeometricKernel_blocks_bddAbove` (#7406): the finite sums of the geometric blocks over the zeros are bounded in trace norm. The Cauchy estimate is proved from it (#7427) with `Isolated.traceClass_blockSums_tails_of_bddAbove`: in the trace class, a family whose finite sums are bounded is unconditionally summable. This is the theorem of Bessaga and Pełczyński for a Banach space that contains no copy of c₀; it was first stated as a classical input and is now proved in Lean directly (#7459). The bound asks for more than the Cauchy estimate: summability in every order. | 464, 466–474, 487–491, 494–496, 503, 519–520, 522 |
| Theorem 11.1.2.1(2)–(3), pp. 481–483: strictness and density for the S-local carvings | I344, I350 | For (3), a proof that the projected local tests are dense in the intersection. For (2) at a fixed degree, a new zero of a reciprocal Euler factor at which a continuous Mellin evaluation does not vanish on a polar-free test of the smaller stage; the basis theorem that is cited has no such degree bound. **Since proved in Lean by another route (#6203, #6273 and others): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 482, 493–496, 519–520 |
| Proposition 11.2.0.1, p. 484, and Theorem 11.2.0.2, pp. 485–486: the orbital Markushevich bases | I288, I290 | Density on the completed zero line on each component, for arbitrary fields and characters (the cited theorem assumes s > 1). Identification of the annihilator of the closed principal-value image with the closed span of the admissible jets, and a total biorthogonal family in the quotient. **Since proved in Lean by another route (#7188, #7253, #7257): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 481–482, 485–487, 489–491, 494–496, 519–520 |
| (11.3.1.3)–(11.3.1.4), p. 494: the Paley–Wiener subquotients of the global stage and the lifts of their bases | M50 | The levels defined on pp. 487–489 are closed subspaces. Identifying a Fourier-support quotient of two successive global θ-images with the orbital basis model, and lifting that basis with estimates in the strip norm, are steps the text leaves out (“We are going to omit all of this from this overview”). In Lean the algebraic comparison is proved at every fixed Fourier bound: the classes of the same tests in the successive quotient and in the single-prime orbital quotient correspond (`primaryDivisorialAbsolutePW_equiv`, #7291). The lifts of the basis with trace-norm estimates are not. | 494–496, 520, 522 |
| Proposition 12.2.2.2(2), p. 508, as used in Theorem 11.3.2.1, p. 495: the individual shell weights | I292 | The printed identities give the integral summed over all translates, while the individual weights vary. The arithmetic shell terms must be identified, or shown to vanish off S. | 495–496, 520, 522; the total on 24, 515–517 |
| §12.3, p. 514: comparison of the global trace with the S-local trace | M25 | The section is a heading followed by “Include section.” Theorem 12.4.1.1 computes a normalized local cutoff trace, while p. 520 needs the residual global trace with two polar Mellin terms subtracted, and both terms are strictly positive for a suitable test. | 519–520, 522 |
| Theorem 12.4.1.1, p. 515: nuclearity, and the reduction of the trace to $E$ | I395 | Step III uses nuclearity to pass to a trace on the diagonal. Lemma C.3.2.2 needs a nuclear factor through $E$ for the truncated operator on the weighted level. None is constructed, and no direct trace-class construction is given there. For the ball cutoff that Step II describes on p. 516, the range of the operator is not contained in the natural image of $E$, already over ℚ with the real place alone, so the factor asserted on p. 515 fails for that cutoff (I218, #7440); for a cutoff with an inner radius, as in Definition 9.3.3.1 and footnote 2 on p. 514, the question is open. | 515–517, 519–520, 522 |
| Theorem 12.4.1.1, pp. 515–517: the kernel on the quotient and its diagonal | I398 | After the input measure is converted, the quotient kernel of the operator has to be constructed and its smoothened diagonal integral identified with the computed sum up to $o(1)$. The proof gives no descended kernel that meets the hypotheses of Proposition 12.2.2.2(2). | 517–520, 522 |
| Theorem 12.5.1.1, (12.5.1.4), p. 520, with Definition 10.4.3.7, p. 473: the diagonal of the smoothened strip kernel | I341, I351 | A weighted diagonal formula for the strip kernel in the composition order A·P, with an integrable limit when the cutoff is removed. Pages 502–503 treat the line kernel and the order P·A, and Theorem 10.4.2.8 compares traces, not diagonals. In Lean the formula is proved from the existence of a projective kernel for A·P, and that existence is stated without proof in a file that nothing imports: `Isolated.exists_projectiveKernel_stripTestAction_spectralProjection` (#7382). | 520, 522 |
| Theorem 12.5.1.1, pp. 519–520: the zeros outside the strip | M25, I292, I341, I351, I353, I354, I368, I369 | For each half-width $\alpha>0$ the theorem equates the local terms with a sum over the zeros inside the strip. The explicit formula (Theorem B.4.4.7) gives the same local terms with the sum over all zeros, so the zeros with $\lvert\Re\rho-1/2\rvert\ge\alpha$ have to contribute zero, for every test vanishing on $C_F^1$. The proof on p. 520 rests on two comparisons that are not carried out: of the smoothened global diagonal with the local terms (M25), and of the same diagonal with the sum over the zeros inside the strip (I341, I351, I368). For a fixed norm section and a fixed $\alpha$, the cancellation holds for all such tests if and only if no completed Hecke L-function of a unitary character trivial on the section has a zero with $\lvert\Re\rho-1/2\rvert\ge\alpha$: `hasSum_exteriorZeros_iff_isEmpty` in `Ch12_TraceGeometric/Sec5.lean` (#5853, CalebAlbers), proved in Lean with nothing open beneath it. The theorem asserts the cancellation for every $\alpha>0$. In the Lean it is the recorded gap `traceFormula_exteriorZeros` (#5571). This is a missing step, not a counterexample to the theorem. Over ℚ the cancellation has since been shown in Lean to be equivalent to Dirichlet GRH (#6991): see “Shown to be as strong as a target” below. | 27–31 and 53–55; p. 522 cites the theorem in a changed form (I370–I372) |
| §1.4.3, p. 29: the separate limit of the spectral terms | F255, R2 | For $F=\mathbf Q$ and the trivial character the real part of the spectral sum at $z=2$ tends to $-\infty$, also along subsequences, so the limit claimed “by a similar reasoning” does not exist. A replacement has to combine or regularize the terms and prove the holomorphy that the deduction needs. **Since proved in Lean from another step of this list (Theorem 12.5.1.1): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 29–30, 54–55, 522 |
| §1.4.5, pp. 29–30: holomorphic continuation of the combined terms | F256, I376 | The calculation on the overlap identifies the combined limit for $\Re z>\max(a,1)$ and gives no holomorphic extension to $\Re z>a$ for each $a>0$. In the Lean this is the statement `introTrace_combined_limit_extension`, from which `heckeL_zero_re_eq_half` is proved. **Since proved in Lean from another step of this list (Theorem 12.5.1.1): see “Steps since proved” below.** | 30, 55, 522, 528 |

## Steps since proved, and steps shown to be as strong as a target

The section above lists steps for which no repair was known. This section records what has
become of such a step since: it has been proved in Lean by an argument that is not the
paper's; or it has been proved in Lean from another step of the list; or it has been proved in
Lean from a published theorem that is stated in Lean and not proved there; or it has been shown
in Lean to be equivalent to a statement the paper sets out to prove. The row in the section
above stays and points here.

An entry is added when the declarations it names are on `main` without `sorry` and
`#print axioms` shows only `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`. The last column
names the vendored projects ([`RiemannMasullo/External/`](RiemannMasullo/External/README.md))
whose code the proof uses, directly or through other declarations of this project.

“Proved by another route” means that the statement holds in the form the Lean states it; each
declaration's docstring marks where that differs from the print. It does not mean that the
printed argument has been repaired.

### Proved in Lean from a published theorem

The statement the errata names for this step is proved in Lean, and the only open statement
beneath it is a theorem of the literature, stated in Lean with the docstring line
`Classical input:` and not proved there. Its declarations therefore still show `sorryAx` under
`#print axioms`, through that statement alone. A classical input is not a gap of the paper, but
it is not checked by Lean either: what is said about its source below is the project's reading.

| Item | What was missing | What is proved | The theorem taken as known | Pull requests |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lemma 5.3.1.5, p. 242: closed embeddings on the θ-quotients (I138, I139) | Closedness of ran P + ker Φ and continuity of the inverse on the image, for both quotients. The relative openness argument on p. 242 controls seminorms before the θ-quotient is taken, not after. | `sLocalThetaPrincipalValue_isClosedEmbedding` (`Ch05_ThetaZSpaces/Sec3a.lean`). The lifting of boundary traces of θ-null tests is reduced to the stage with one finite place removed. There an identity for the valuation shells is proved, and the difference of the identity and a norm-changing translation is shown to be open onto its image on θ-classes: the θ-quotient decomposes by finite conductor; the Mellin transforms of the divided tests are dominated by those of a bounded sequence; uniform Schwartz bounds on vertical lines follow from that by the Phragmén–Lindelöf principle and Cauchy's estimate; and the classical input turns those bounds into bounded classes. | `sLocalMellinSchwartzBounds_meyer_infinite_span_outside_real_profiles`. The criterion says that a family of θ-classes has representatives in one bounded set of Schwartz–Bruhat functions exactly when the Mellin transforms of the θ-functions of the tests, and of their Fourier transforms, satisfy uniform Schwartz bounds on every line of real part greater than one. Lean proves that bounded classes give the bounds, and the converse when the classes span a finite-dimensional space or are given by even real Schwartz profiles, which covers ℚ with no finite place (#7434). The statement taken as known is the rest of the converse. Source: R. Meyer, *On a representation of the idele class group related to primes and zeros of L-functions*, Duke Math. J. 127 (2005), arXiv:math/0311468: Corollary 4.17 (the coinvariants of the S-units on the Schwartz–Bruhat space of the S-adeles are the space T(C_S), by summation over the S-units) and Lemma 4.20 at α = 1 (boundedness in T(C_S) is detected outside the critical strip). The paper states this for bornological spaces and on the idele classes; writing it on the dual group, with this library's Schwartz–Bruhat space, Fourier transform and measures, is the project's work and is not proved in Lean. The integrator and an independent reviewer checked the whole criterion against the paper (#7346); no specialist in the paper's framework has. | #7213, #7258, #7284, #7307, #7311, #7329, #7331, #7346 (the project's agents), #7434 (mlyle) |

### Shown to be as strong as a target

| Item | What is proved | Pull request | Vendored code used |
|---|---|---|---|
| Theorem 12.5.1.1, pp. 519–520: the zeros outside the strip (the recorded gap `traceFormula_exteriorZeros`, #5571) | Over ℚ, the cancellation of the exterior zeros, with its quantifiers over norm sections, positive half-widths and tests vanishing on the norm-one classes, holds if and only if Dirichlet GRH holds, in the form of the target `DirichletLNonvanishingRightOfHalf`: `traceFormula_exteriorZeros_rat_iff_dirichletGRH` (`Ch13_ZerosHecke/RationalExteriorZeros.lean`). | #6991 (adolfogc) | algebraic-number-theory-lean: the rational idelic Dirichlet characters |
| Theorem 12.5.1.1, pp. 519–520, with §1.4.5, p. 30: the zeros outside the strip, over every number field | For a number field and one section of its idele class norm: at a half-width a, the exterior terms cancel for every test vanishing on the norm-one classes if and only if every unitary Hecke character of the field has all its zeros of the critical strip in the open window \|Re s − 1/2\| < a (`introTrace_exterior_cancellation_iff_all_zeros_in_window`). This includes the characters that are nontrivial on the section. For a set of positive half-widths that comes arbitrarily close to zero, cancellation at all of them holds if and only if every such zero has real part 1/2 (`introTrace_cofinal_exterior_cancellation_iff_critical_line`). Both are in `Ch01_Introduction/Sec4.lean`. | #7211 (mlyle), with the fixed-width theorem of #5853 (CalebAlbers) | none |

| F200 | Theorems 10.4.3.4(2) and 10.4.3.6(2), second kernels in (10.4.3.30), (10.4.3.47), pp. 470–471, 473 | The cutoff times the weighted **spectral** kernel represents “the operator $P^{\mathrm{geom}}_{\rho,a}\circ A(\varphi)$” on p. 471 and “the operator $M_\rho\circ A(\varphi)\circ P_a^{\mathrm{geom}}$” on p. 473. | Take $F=\mathbf Q$, $s=4$, $(a,\alpha)=(2,0)$ or $(3,1)$ respectively. This moment has dense kernel since a correcting bump can be moved to $-\infty$ with norm tending to zero. The translation series has a norm-convergent Möbius inverse since $a>1+\alpha$ and translation by $\log n$ has norm $O(n^\alpha(1+\log^2 n))$. Thus the geometric space contains the radial subspace. Radial convolution kills its orthogonal complement, so the spectral convolution is zero in either order. Take $\Lambda=2$, $\varepsilon=1/8$, $\rho=1_{[1/2,2]}(\lvert\cdot\rvert)$, and the same even nonnegative smooth log-norm bump, positive at zero, for test and input. | F213 | Lemma 5.3.1.1, p. 240 (formerly R7) | The operator P_v(f) := f − ρ_v(π_v, f) on 𝒮(F_v), that is f(x) − f(π_v⁻¹x), "does not depend on the choice of π_v, and it is a linear topological embedding. | F214 | Theorem 10.3.1.1, proof, the constant $C$ after (10.3.1.18), p. 451 (formerly R5) | “$C$ is such that $\lVert\eta_{\chi_k,t_{k,j},m'_{k,j}}\rVert_{-s,2}\cdot\lVert\psi_{\chi_k,t_{k,j},m_{k,j}}\rVert_{s,2}\le C$”, and “$C$ can be chosen to be independent of $m'_{k,j}$ and of $(k,j)$”: the set of the $\eta$ in $W^{-s,2}(C_F)$ “and its bi-orthogonal in $W^{s,2}(C_F)$ are both contained in the closed ball of radius $C^{1/2}$”. | Take $F=\mathbf Q$, $\chi=1$, $a=1/2$. For two zeros $1/2+it$, $1/2+iu$ of $\zeta$ the functionals $\eta_t=\lvert g\rvert^{it}$ and $\eta_u=\lvert g\rvert^{iu}$ have the same norm, and $\lVert\eta_t-\eta_u\rVert_{-s,2}^2=\int_{\mathbf R}\lvert e^{i(t-u)x}-1\rvert^2(1+x^2)^{-s/2}\,dx$ tends to $0$ with $\lvert t-u\rvert$, since $s>1$. Biorthogonality gives $1=\langle\psi_t,\eta_t-\eta_u\rangle_{s,2}\le\lVert\psi_t\rVert_{s,2}\,\lVert\eta_t-\eta_u\rVert_{-s,2}$. There are $\gg T\log T$ simple zeros on the critical line up to height $T$, so distinct ordinates come arbitrarily close and the vectors $\psi_t$ are unbounded. Proposition 10.2.1.3, cited for $C$, bounds only the $\eta$. | F215 | Theorem 11.1.2.1(2), strictness in (11.1.2.6), p. 480; proof, (11.1.2.16), p. 482 (formerly R6) | “for any non-decreasing sequence $\{\Lambda_k\}_{k\ge0}\subset\mathbf R_{>0}$ such that $\lim_{k\ge0}\Lambda_k=+\infty$, there exists a subsequence $\{\Lambda_{k_m}\}_{m\ge0}$ such that, for every integer $m\ge0$ and for the choice $\Lambda=\Lambda_{k_m}$, the inclusion in (11.1.2.6) is strict.” | Take $F=\mathbf Q$, $\chi=1$, $S=\{\infty\}$, $S_i=\varnothing$, $S_{i'}=\{p\}$ and any $s\ge2$. On the trivial isotype the functions depend on $\lvert g\rvert$, and the principal value at $p$ is $I-T$ with $T$ a translation by $\log p$ in $\log\lvert g\rvert$. The space $\mathrm H=\mathrm H_{0,\mathrm{cont}}(1,\mathcal S_0(\mathbf A_{\mathbf Q}))$ is stable under $T$ and $T^{-1}$. For $h\in\mathrm H$ the telescoping sums $h-T^{N}h=(I-T)\sum_{0\le j<N}T^{j}h$ and $h-T^{-N}h=-(I-T)\sum_{1\le j\le N}T^{-j}h$ lie in $(I-T)\mathrm H$. Choose the sign for which $T^{\pm N}h$ takes at $g$ the value of $h$ at norm $p^N\lvert g\rvert$. For either cutoff $\rho$, once $p^N\ge\Lambda^2$, $\lVert M_\rho T^{\pm N}h\rVert_{s,2}^2$ is at most the tail $\int_{\lvert g\rvert>p^N/\Lambda}\lvert h\rvert^2(1+\lvert\log\lvert g\rvert\rvert^2)^{s/2}\,\mathrm d\bar\mu$ and tends to $0$. So $M_\rho h$ lies in the closure of $M_\rho(I-T)\mathrm H$ and the two closed cutoff spaces are equal for every $\Lambda$. The proof on p. 482 infers equality in (11.1.2.5) from the equalities (11.1.2.16) because $M_{\rho_{\Lambda_k,+}}\to1$ strongly, but the approximating vectors depend on $k$. | `sLocalCutoffCarving_le` states the inclusion and permits equality. The printed claim is kept as `Printed.sLocalCutoffCarving_strictSubsequence`; its refutation `Printed.sLocalCutoffCarving_strictSubsequence_false` is stated and its proof is open. | issue #1170; also reported by Naslund | reviewed |
| F216 | Proposition 12.2.2.2(4), last sentence, p. 509; proof, p. 511 (formerly R8) | “The point $(x,y)\in G\times G$ is regular for $\Psi^\Delta_{u_0}(u)$ if and only if $(\pi(x),\pi(y))\in G/H\times G/H$ is regular for $u$.” | The equivalence fails at single points. Take additive $G=\mathbb Z\times\mathbb R$, $H=\mathbb Z\times\{0\}$, $D=\{0\}\times\mathbb R$, $U_k=\{0\}\times(-2^{-k},2^{-k})$. Let $a(t)=\tfrac34+\tfrac14\operatorname{sgn}(t)\min(1,\lvert t\rvert^{1/8})$ and let the indentation $u_0$ be $a(t)$ on the slice $0$, $1-a(t)$ on the slice $1$ and zero on the others. On $G/H=\mathbb R$ take the rank-one kernel $u(t,s)=g(t)\chi(s)$ with $\chi(t)=\max(0,\min(1,2-\lvert t\rvert))$, $g(t)=\chi(t)\operatorname{sgn}(t)\lvert t\rvert^{-1/4}$ and $g(0)=0$. It is locally integrable and regular at every point of its diagonal; at the origin its averages vanish because $g$ is odd. Near the origin of $G\times G$ the lift is $u$ times $a(t)a(s)+(1-a(t))(1-a(s))$, and its averages over the squares of radius $r=2^{-k}$ equal $r^{-1/8}/7$ and diverge. | F227 | Proposition C.2.3.13(3), p. 683 (formerly I18) | Proposition C.2.3.13(3): "For Hausdorff locally convex topological C-vector spaces E and F, if they are barrelled respectively bornological then so is" the completed inductive tensor product of E and F. | The bornological half fails. With second factor ℂ the completed inductive tensor product is the completion of E, and the completion of a bornological space need not be bornological. Let S be a stationary subset of ω₁ with stationary complement, X = S ∪ {ω₁} as a subspace of ω₁ + 1, and E = C(X) with the compact-open topology. X is Lindelöf, hence realcompact, so E is bornological by the Nachbin–Shirota theorem. S contains no closed unbounded subset of ω₁, so every compact subset of X meets S in a bounded set, and the completion of E is the space of all functions on X that are continuous on S, with compact convergence; it is C(S) × ℂ. Every continuous function on S is eventually constant (pressing-down lemma), and the eventual value is a linear functional on C(S) that is bounded on bounded sets and not continuous. The barrelled half holds. | `completedInductiveTensor_barrelled` proves the barrelled half. The bornological half is not stated. | F235 | Theorem 4.6.1.5, hypothesis and (4.6.1.15)–(4.6.1.17), p. 212 | With $b_k:=\log(\lvert\gamma_k\rvert_G)$: “Assume that there exists a real number $b\in\mathbf R_{>1}$ such that $\log(b_k)\in\mathbf Q\cdot\log(b)$ for every $1\le k\le N$.” Then every enumeration of the functionals (4.6.1.15) is an unconditional Schauder basis of the strong dual of the quotient, the biorthogonal set (4.6.1.16) “is an absolute Schauder basis”, and $q(\psi_{\xi,m})\ll_{q,k}p(n_{\xi,k})$ for a polynomial $p$. | The counterexample uses $G=\mathbf R_{>0}$, $\chi=1$, $q_1=10$, $q_{j+1}=10^{q_j^4}$, $B=1+\sum_jq_j^{-1}$ and $\gamma_1=e^B,\ \gamma_2=e^{B^2}$. | F239 | Theorem 10.3.1.1(2), (10.3.1.3)–(10.3.1.4), p. 448 | For real $a$ and $s>1$, $A(\varphi)$ is nuclear on $\mathscr H^s_{F,a}$ and $\mathrm{Trace}\,A(\varphi)=\sum_{\chi}\sum_{t\in\mathbf R:L(a+it,\chi)=0}N_s(\rho_{\chi,t})\int_{C_F}\varphi(g)\chi\lvert g\rvert_F^{it}\,\mathrm d\bar\mu(g)$ with $N_s(\rho_{\chi,t})=\min\{N(\rho_{\chi,t}),[\frac{s-1}{2}+1]\}$. | The counterexample uses the splitting field $F$ of $X^3-2$ and $K=\mathbf Q(\zeta_3)$. | F240 | Theorem 10.3.2.1(2), (10.3.2.3)–(10.3.2.4), p. 454 | $A(\varphi)$ is nuclear on $\mathscr H^s_{F,a,I}$, $I=[-\alpha,\alpha]$, and $\mathrm{Trace}\,A(\varphi)=\sum_\chi\sum_{t\in\mathbf R,b\in[-\alpha,\alpha]:L(a+b+it,\chi)=0}N_{I,s}(\rho_{\chi,t,b})\int_{C_F}\varphi(g)\chi\lvert g\rvert_F^{b+it}\,\mathrm d\bar\mu(g)$ with $N_{I,s}=\min\{N,[\frac{s-1}{2}+1]\}$ at $b=\pm\alpha$. | A zero $\beta+i\gamma$ of order at least two of the Dedekind zeta function of the splitting field of $X^3-2$ (as in F152) lies on the upper edge when $s=3$, $\alpha>0$ and $a=\beta-\alpha$. The radial test is $V^{-1}e^{-i\gamma x}v(x)$ for a nonzero nonnegative smooth bump $v$ in $(1,2)$, with $x$ the log norm and $V$ the norm-one volume. Its multiplier $K(b+it)=\int v(y)e^{(b+i(t-\gamma))y}\,dy$ reaches its maximum modulus $R=K(\alpha+i\gamma)$ only at that edge point. Rapid vertical decay and the zero count give dominated convergence. Under the printed formula the traces of the convolution powers divided by $R^n$ therefore tend to $2$. Thus $g$ is tempered. The characteristic function of this density has modulus one only at frequency zero, so $\lambda=R$ and $g$ is a polynomial. The positive-tail norm allows only constants. | F246 | Theorem 12.4.1.1, Step II, pp. 516–517, Fourier cutoff (12.4.1.8) | The bullet gives “$\widehat P_{\rho,\lambda'}:\mathcal S(\mathbf A_{F,S})\to\mathcal S(\mathbf A_{F,S})$” using the smoothed product-norm ball in Fourier space. | Take $\mathbf Q$, $S=\{\infty,p\}$ and $f=b''\otimes1_{\mathbf Z_p^\times}$, $0\ne b\in C_c^\infty(1,2)$. This belongs to the smaller space (5.3.4.1), with all real jets zero at 0 and integral zero. Its Fourier transform has a nonzero entire real factor and finite factor $1_{\mathbf Z_p}-p^{-1}1_{p^{-1}\mathbf Z_p}$, constant $1-p^{-1}$ on $\mathbf Z_p$. Let $m$ be a continuous upper cutoff with distinct plateaux. Choose $a>0$ and an integer $d>0$ with $m(a)\ne m(p^da)$. For any proposed period subgroup $p^N\mathbf Z_p$, compare $p^k,p^{k+d}$ with $k\ge\max(N,0)$ at real frequencies near $p^{k+d}a$. The multipliers differ on an interval on which the entire factor cannot vanish identically. Every additive Schwartz–Bruhat function has a common finite-place period, and Fourier inversion preserves that space. R9 gives the spatial multiplier example. | `sLocalTraceFourierCutoff` returns a scalar function. `Printed.sLocalProductCutoff_preservesSchwartzBruhat` records the spatial obstruction of R9. The printed claim is kept as `Printed.sLocalTraceFourierCutoff_preservesSchwartzBruhat`. Proved in Lean from statements that are still unproved, so not yet a checked refutation: `Printed.sLocalProductCutoff_preservesSchwartzBruhat_false` (#6065), `Printed.sLocalTraceFourierCutoff_preservesSchwartzBruhat_false` (#6082). | F247 | Theorem 13.3.1.1, capped critical-line trace (13.3.1.1)–(13.3.1.5), p. 522 | For $\varphi$ satisfying (13.3.1.2) “upon choosing a continuous homomorphic section $\tau$ to the idelic norm”, the sum $\sum_{c:L(c)=0,\sigma(c)=1/2}N_{\mathrm{spec}}(c)\int_{C_F}\varphi(g)c(g)\lvert g\rvert_F^{-1/2}\,\mathrm d\bar\mu(g)$ with $N_{\mathrm{spec}}(c):=\min\{N(c),[F:\mathbf Q]\}$ equals the right side of (13.3.1.3)–(13.3.1.5). | The example uses $E:y^2=x^3-n^2x$ with $n=1254$. On $E(\mathbf Q)$ the two-isogeny descent map sends a point with nonzero abscissa to its square class, and sends $(0,0)$ to the class of $-n^2$. Thus $(-1058,21896),(-912,25992),(0,0),(1254,0)$ give $-2,-57,-1,1254$. Direct substitution verifies both points with nonzero ordinate. Valuations at $11,19,2$, followed by the sign, give independence in $\mathbf Q^\times/\mathbf Q^{\times2}$. Full rational two-torsion then gives rank at least two. The root number is $-1$ since $n\equiv6\bmod8$. Gross–Zagier and Kolyvagin exclude analytic rank one, so CM gives a unitary Hecke character $\chi$ over $\mathbf Q(i)$ with a zero of order $m\ge3$ at $1/2$ and angular weight $\omega=\pm1$. With finite section components $1$ and complex component $\sqrt t\,e^{i\log t}$ we have $\chi(\tau(t))=t^{i\omega}$. For nonzero $0\le h\in C_c^\infty(1,2)$ the construction uses $b=(d/dx-i\omega)^2h$, $B(z)=(z+i\omega)^2\int h(x)e^{zx}\,dx$, and $\varphi_u=\bar\chi\,b(\log\lvert g\rvert_K-u)$, $u\ge0$. For a character $\chi\lvert\cdot\rvert^{iv}$ its Mellin test is $Ve^{(s+iv)u}B(s+iv)$, with a double zero at the required $s=-i(\omega+v)$. The other compact types vanish by orthogonality. Yet $B(0)\ne0$. The full-divisor explicit formula uses Fourier-defined local distributions and the global $1/V$ normalization of (B.4.4.16). The polar terms vanish by compact-character orthogonality. Taking the difference with the full zero sum on the positive side and dividing out its common Haar factor gives, for $\lambda=\rho-1/2$, $\sum_\lambda d_\lambda B(\lambda)e^{\lambda u}=O(e^{-u/2})$, with $d_\lambda=m_\lambda-V\mathbf1_{\Re\lambda=0}\min(m_\lambda,2)$ for a zero of order $m_\lambda$. In particular $d_0=m-2V$, with $V=\pi/4$ from (A.3.2.1), p. 560. Only the complex place and the place above $2$ can have different local weights. On $u+1<\log\lvert g\rvert_K<u+2$ the local densities contribute $O(e^{-u/2})$ against bounded tests on annuli of bounded multiplicative mass. The Laplace transform of this error is holomorphic for $\Re w>-1/2$. The zero sum continues there as $\sum_\lambda d_\lambda B(\lambda)/(w-\lambda)$, locally normally away from the zeros by rapid vertical decay and the Hecke zero count. Its residue $(m-2V)B(0)$ at zero is nonzero. With matched normalizations the residue is $(m-2)B(0)$ as well, up to a common nonzero scale. The test for the rotating section fails the zeroth-moment condition for the straight section. Rebuilding the test for that section gives $b=h''$ and a transform that does vanish at zero. [Elkies, root-number formula for congruent-number curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0208056) | `Printed.criticalTraceFormula` keeps the capped critical-line equality and its `_false` proof is `sorry`. `hasSum_fullDivisor_on_criticalTraceTests` states the full-divisor identity with canonical measures on the jet test subspace (§13.3). | Goyal, *Audit of Masullo's proposed proof*, `report/report.md`, “What survives correcting all measures”; `dossiers/notes/trace.md`, “The choice of norm section can expose a multiple central zero” (the audit credits the construction to the revision by Titulus233, folios 522f–522h) | reviewed |

The trace at the right endpoint is $i/y$ for $y\ne0$ and fails $L^2$ near zero. | `scalarIntervalSobolev_mellin_holomorphic`, `scalarIntervalSobolev_boundary_values` and `scalarIntervalSobolev_boundary_traces` in `RiemannMasullo/Ch09_HardySobolev/Sec2.lean` use data controlled at both endpoints. See F233 for parts (1) and (3). | wave 1 statement review | reviewed |
| F273 | Corollaries 9.2.2.8(2), (9.2.2.30), p. 416, and 9.2.2.12(2), p. 418 | For even $s$, Corollary 9.2.2.8(2) gives an if-and-only-if bound $0\le k\le[(s-1)/2]$ at the finite upper endpoint. Its source is implicit after the construction on p. 415. Corollary 9.2.2.12(2) explicitly asserts continuity in that range at every boundary point on the supported analytic tensor space. | Sample only interior lines for $I=[-1,0]$. Take $s=2$, $k=0$, cutoff $A=2$ and a smooth unit-integral bump $h$ in $(0,1)$. The translates $h(x-n)$ meet the support cutoff. Their transformed derivative norms of fixed order $j$ are $O((1+n)^j e^{\sigma n})$ on each fixed interior line $\sigma$, while their values at zero stay 1. The trivial norm-one subgroup gives the tensor case. | `scalarIntervalSobolev_cutoff_eval_boundary_iff`, `scalarIntervalSobolev_cutoff_eval_endpoints_iff` and `modulatedIntervalSobolev_cutoff_eval_endpoints_iff` in `RiemannMasullo/Ch09_HardySobolev/Sec2.lean` use the physical endpoint norm, with criterion $\Re z<b$ or $2k+1<s$. See F237 for unrestricted evaluation. | wave 1 statement review | reviewed |
| F274 | Propositions 9.2.1.8, (9.2.1.26–28), pp. 409–411, and 9.2.2.11, (9.2.2.37–39), pp. 417–418 | “For every open interval $J\subset\mathbf R$ containing $I$,” the bilinear integral pairs all of $\mathcal S_J\times\mathcal S_J$. | Set $s=0$, $J=(1,2)$ and $I=[5/4,7/4]$. In log radius take $f(x)=e^{-x}$ for $x<-1$, smoothly cut to zero by $x=0$. Each $e^{cx}f$ is Schwartz for $c\in J$, but the self-pairing $\int f^2\,dx$ diverges. The scalar group is also a case of the group proposition. | `scalarIntervalSobolev_dual` and `modulatedIntervalSobolev_dual` in `RiemannMasullo/Ch09_HardySobolev/Sec2.lean` pair $L^2(w\,d\mu)$ with $L^2(w^{-1}\,d\mu)$. | wave 1 statement review | reviewed |
| F275 | Propositions 9.2.1.8, (9.2.1.29), p. 409, and 9.2.2.11, (9.2.2.40), p. 417 | The dual space is the completion of the entire second copy of $\mathcal S_J$ in the reciprocal norm. | For the same $s=0$, $J=(1,2)$ and $I=[5/4,7/4]$, a smooth negative-tail cutoff of $e^{-x}$ lies in $\mathcal S_J$. On $x<-1$ the primal weight is $e^{(5/2)x}$, so the squared reciprocal norm integrates $e^{-(9/2)x}$ there and diverges. | The dual branch of `CompactIntervalSobolev` in `RiemannMasullo/Ch09_HardySobolev/Sec2.lean` uses $L^2(w^{-1}\,d\mu)$ data. `scalarIntervalSobolev_dual` and `modulatedIntervalSobolev_dual` identify this space with the strong dual. | wave 1 statement review | reviewed |
| F276 | Definition 9.3.1.1, (9.3.1.1–2), p. 419 | It sets $A(\varphi)=\int_G\varphi(g)\rho_G(g,\cdot)\,d\mu_G(g)$ in $\mathcal L_\beta(E)$, “where the integral is in the sense of Pettis.” | On unweighted log-line $L^2$, the norm-closed span of translations consists of operators with uniformly almost periodic multipliers. A nonzero smooth compactly supported kernel has a nonzero Fourier multiplier vanishing at infinity. Hahn–Banach gives an operator functional vanishing on all translations and detecting that multiplier. Its scalar integral is zero, yet vector evaluations force any operator Pettis integral to equal the convolution. | `bruhatRegularizedAction` and `bruhatRegularizedAction_apply` in `RiemannMasullo/Ch09_HardySobolev/Sec3.lean` integrate each vector orbit in $E$. | wave 1 statement review | reviewed |
| F277 | Lemma 9.3.2.1, (9.3.2.1), pp. 422–423 | The test action extends continuously to $A:\mathcal S(G)\to\mathcal B_{\mathrm{NT}}(W^s_{\{b\}}(G))$ for every real $b$. | For $b=1$, $s=0$ on the positive reals, let $h,u$ be nonzero nonnegative smooth log-radius bumps. The kernels $e^{-n/2}h(x-n)$ tend to zero in ordinary Schwartz space. Their convolutions with $u$ have $L^2(e^{2x}dx)$ norms $e^{n/2}\lVert h*u\rVert_{L^2(e^{2x}dx)}$. | `existsUnique_singletonDecay_lineAction` in `RiemannMasullo/Ch09_HardySobolev/Sec3.lean` uses kernels whose $b$-twists are Schwartz–Bruhat. `existsUnique_schwartzBruhat_zeroLineAction` gives the ordinary-source extension at $b=0$. | wave 1 statement review | reviewed |

| I32 | (A.5.1.2) and Theorem A.5.1.1(1), pp. 566–567 | (A.5.1.2) defines Mf with "lim sup" over the subscript r > 0, which is neither the supremum over r > 0 nor the limit superior as r → 0⁺. With the supremum the finiteness claim of part (1) fails: f(x) = \|x\| satisfies both local hypotheses, and its centred average over a radius r > \|x\| is (r² + x²)/(2r), which is unbounded, so Mf is infinite at every point. With the limit superior as r → 0⁺ the three parts hold, because that function depends only on f near the point and is bounded by the supremum; this weaker form is all that the proof of Theorem A.5.1.3 (p. 568) uses, through ω_g ≤ 2Mg. | Measurability and the estimates (2)–(3) are stated. | #607 | reviewed |

| I49 | Lemma D.1.9.3, p. 781 | The statement speaks of "a left action on the completion $\widehat E$" and does not say that the action is linear. The proof uses linearity (it splits $W'.x$ as $W'.(x-x_0)+W'.x_0$), and the word is missing in Lemmas D.1.9.1 and D.1.9.4 as well. Linear actions are evidently meant (the proof of D.1.9.1, p. 780, writes the action as a homomorphism into $\mathcal L(E)$), and for them the lemma holds. Read literally it fails: on $E=\mathbf C\oplus c_{00}$, with completion $\mathbf C\oplus\ell^2$, let the additive group $H$ of bounded continuous functions $\psi:\ell^2\to\mathbf R$ act by the shears $\psi.(z,v)=(z+\psi(v),v)$, with the topology of uniform convergence on a ball around each $v\in c_{00}$, the balls kept at positive distance from a fixed $x\in\ell^2\setminus c_{00}$. Each $\psi$ acts by a homeomorphism and the action is continuous on $H\times E$, but bumps of height 1 at $x$ with supports shrinking to $x$ tend to zero in $H$ and move $(0,x)$ to $(1,x)$. | Linearity is a hypothesis, here and in D.1.9.1 and D.1.9.4 (`continuous_action_completion`, `continuous_action_strict_inductive_limit`, `continuous_action_projectiveTensor`). | #811 | reviewed |
| I50 | Lemmas D.1.6.6 and D.1.6.7, p. 767 | The proof of D.1.6.6 fixes a structural pair $(k,\Gamma)$ "such that $f$ is supported on $\Gamma$", and the proof of D.1.6.7 says that "$\Gamma$ contains the support of $f$". Definition D.1.5.3(1), p. 762, puts no support condition on $C^p(G)$ or $C^\infty(G)$: on discrete $\mathbf Q$ the constant function 1 is smooth by that definition (with $k$ trivial), its support is $\mathbf Q$, and every open compactly generated subgroup of $\mathbf Q$ is finitely generated, hence proper. Remark D.1.5.5, p. 763, defines $C^\infty(G)$ a second time, as the colimit of the spaces $C^\infty(\Gamma/k)$. That space has such supports, but it is a different space (it does not contain the constant function on $\mathbf Q$), and $C^1(G)$ has only the first definition. The conclusions of both lemmas hold for Definition D.1.5.3(1) when $\Gamma$ is chosen around each point. | Both lemmas are stated for the functions of Definition D.1.5.3(1), with a structural pair chosen around each point in place of a common support (`bruhatDirectionalDeriv_continuous`, `bruhatDirectionalDeriv_mul`, `bruhatFirstOrder_schwartz`, `bruhatFirstOrder_commute`). See the report on #18. | #811 | reviewed |
| I51 | Remark C.4.2.2, pp. 710–711 | The Bochner and Pettis integrals are said to coincide "when the function is continuous and weakly integrable on a locally compact Hausdorff Radon space and valued in a separable Banach space". Such a function need not have a Bochner integral. On $\mathbb N$ with $\mu(\{n\})=2^{-n-1}$, the function $f(n)=2^{n+1}e_n/(n+1)$ into $\ell^2$ is integrable against every functional and has Pettis integral $(1/(n+1))_n$, but the integral of its norm is the harmonic series. The two integrals coincide when both exist, as the next sentence of the remark says. | The Bochner-to-Pettis direction is stated. | #868 | reviewed |

The inequality (C.4.3.9), $\nu\big(\int_X f\,\mathrm d\mu\big)\le\int_X\nu(f(x))\,\mathrm d\mu(x)$, has a complex right-hand side with no order; for $\mu=-\delta$ on a point and $f=1$ it reads $1\le-1$. In (3.3.2.17) the complex phase for signed angular weight $r$ is $i^{\lvert r\rvert}$. The residues of the **zeta integral applied to a test function** are $2f^{(n)}(0)/n!$ at a matching real degree and $2\pi$ times the coefficient of $z^{m+\max(-r,0)}\bar z^{m+\max(r,0)}$ in the complex Taylor jet at $s=-\lvert r\rvert/2-m$. | M19 | Theorem 6.1.2.1(2), pp. 288, 295 | The proof ends “the range of $N_G$, and hence of $\bar\pi_{S,S'}$, is $\mathrm H^0(\widehat{\mathcal O}_{F,S'-S}^\times,\mathrm H_{0,\mathrm{cont}}(\mathcal O_{F,S'}^\times,\mathcal S(\mathbf A_{F,S'})))$, as desired.” Part (2) concerns $\bar\pi_S$. Represent a global fixed class at a finite stage $T\supsetneq S$. Equivariance and injectivity into the global space give a class fixed by the units at $T-S$. Part (1) then gives a representative from $S$. Lean states `sLocalThetaToGlobal_range_eq_invariants`. | #2208, #3849 | reviewed |
| M20 | Theorem 7.1.2.3, proof, p. 321, footnote 1 (cf. the last paragraph of §7.1.1, p. 315) | “check if Definition B.3.1.1 is actually applicable since no control on growth of $\lvert T_k\rvert$’s in Lemma 7.1.1.1”. Escape gives no polynomial rate. For the repaired residue theorem (F17), choose $T_k\in(k,k+1/2)$ a fixed distance from the finitely many pole lattices. Each interval meets uniformly few lattice points even after enlargement, so deleting short neighborhoods leaves a choice. This gives linear growth on both tails and uniform denominator bounds on the strip. The vertical sides must also be pole-free with integrable Mellin traces. | #2211, #3838 | reviewed |

| M39 | Theorem 12.4.1.1, p. 514 n. 2, referring to (12.4.1.23–24), p. 518, | The note calls for an independent inner radius and says “Then study the convergence of the integral in (12.4.1.23)”. The change matters already for $\mathbf Q$, $S=\{\infty\}$, with a smooth nonnegative radial bump supported in $(1,2)$. Put $T=\lambda\lambda'$, $c=\lambda'/\lambda''$. The inner interval starts at $\max(\lvert w\rvert/\lambda',1/\lambda'')$. For $\lvert w\rvert\le T$ the normalized logarithmic volume loses $\log_+(c/\lvert w\rvert)$. As $c\downarrow0$ its integral against the bounded Fourier amplitude costs $O(c)$. The upper-only limit is positive for the bump. As $c\to\infty$, the volume is $\log(T/c)$ on $\lvert w\rvert\le c$. Fourier inversion gives total Fourier mass proportional to $\varphi(1)=0$. The residual tail is bounded by $\int_{\lvert w\rvert>c}\lvert\widehat\eta(w)\rvert\log(\lvert w\rvert/c)\,\mathrm dw\to0$, since there are just the two real $S$-units and Schwartz amplitudes. The paths $(\lambda,\lambda',\lambda'')=(n^2,n,n^2)$ and $(n,n^2,n)$ have the same $T=n^3$ and give $c=1/n$ and $c=n$, respectively. Both allow $\varepsilon=n^{-4}$. The proposed annular amendment needs a scale condition or its boundary term. R3 already reports a two-place order-of-limits example. | issue #2469, item 14 | reviewed |
